# Integration automation coordinator status

## Reset to coordinator and release owner

Read this card and the active gate in [sprint_roadmap.md](sprint_roadmap.md)
after every reset and before changing approach. Name the next acceptance
result and use the simplest existing supported path that can prove it.
Add code, checks, or process only for a stated acceptance criterion or a
concrete failure with evidence. Save optional hardening without delaying the
current gate. If a blocker repeats, give independent QA the smallest repro
and one decision to resolve. Apply the smallest accepted fix and return to
the live path. Preserve protected data and independent acceptance criteria.

The owner runs this alone and rarely adds companies. A new Company may wait
for the next manual scrape. The priority is automatic Jetson wrapper
generation when a scrape finds a failed or partial supported integration.
Keep one Jetson lane, existing Python coordination, MuleLab validation and
the bounded release/retry path. Do not delay wrapper generation to optimize
immediate Company dispatch or support multiple editors.

## Reset rule for repeated blockers

After every reset and during execution, escalate unresolved architecture or
release-safety decisions and investigations that are repeating without new
evidence. Follow up with the assigned higher-reasoning ChatGPT reviewer, or
send a direct review assignment if the question has no owner. Include the
decision needed, smallest reproducer, prior attempts, affected version, and
protected requirements. Save the assignment, evidence, and next action here.
Continue independent work, verify the advice, and keep independent QA as the
release gate. See [sprint_roadmap.md](sprint_roadmap.md).

Worker reporting rule for all lanes. If Codex flags a turn or cannot display
the response, stop the affected check and alert the coordinator promptly.
Save a short blocker in the assigned handoff with the time, visible error,
and acceptance evidence still outstanding. If the turn ends before the
worker can report, record the blocker as the first step on resume. The
coordinator must also check chat status for silent failures, record any
missing QA as pending, and include this rule in future worker assignments.
Use a narrower in-scope follow-up only when appropriate. Avoid repeat retries.

Escalation checkpoint. GPT-6.2 Astra High returned the terminal disposition
design in chat `01a1f97d-cebd-7910-8d31-a5f3e0dcd264`. See
[the saved decision](S4_SELECTED_RETRY_TERMINAL_DESIGN_20261120.md). The
coordinator owns implementation. Independent QA owns acceptance.

## Active coordinator checkpoint — 2026-11-20 F3/F4 independent rejections

- Role: senior engineering coordinator and release owner. The persistent goal
  remains all six milestones plus final acceptance. Overall completion is
  approximately **25% by acceptance gates**; prior 80% statements measured
  apparent implementation/offline coverage and were not a valid roadmap
  completion measure.

  admission, partial-publication recovery, and retry-outcome provenance
  boundaries passed independent source QA and are backed up through GitHub
  commit `ca7b658` on
  `backup/integration-automation-m1-operator-20261120`.
- F3 independent verdict: **REJECT / OPEN**. The normal scraper lock excludes
  concurrent file installation, but `runner.py` imports application modules
  before acquiring that lock. A suspended old process can therefore resume
  after activation and combine old loaded modules with new lazily imported
  files. Existing tests contend the lock but do not exercise the real
  pre-import startup path. Additional gaps include replay of an old completed
  release receipt while another release is active, missing under-lock Company
  revision recheck, non-idempotent rollback receipt recovery, and an
  undocumented writer/scraper lock order.
- F4 independent verdict: **REJECT / OPEN**. Fifty-two mock-based tests pass,
  but the MuleLab coordinator sends request ID/fingerprint/owner-generation
  controls that the current reviewed Jetson wrapper source rejects. No current
  reviewed authenticated wrapper-control status or POST-only orphan-recovery
  route was found. A real lost transport would remain UNCERTAIN rather than
  proving retained-result reconciliation. No live F4 test is authorized until
  that source contract is repaired and independently reviewed.
- Current focus and ownership: coordinator owns the MuleLab F3 bootstrap,
  measured-version and crash-recovery repair; independent QA owns adversarial
  subprocess review after implementation. The F4 adapter remains a separate
  later lane requiring the mandatory Jetson documentation/change process.
- Safety state: the durable Samtec request remains held, the historical
  Saronic conflict remains untouched, and broad backlog processing plus
  unqualified automatic releases remain disabled. No live scrape, Google
  write, release, or Jetson mutation occurred in these reviews.
- Next action: implement a stable pre-import admission bootstrap and exact
  active-release/runtime pin, then add real subprocess barriers for startup,
  activation, and rollback in both orderings. Do not claim F3 from unit lock
  contention alone.

## Mandatory reread points

Read this card at the beginning of every resumed coordinator turn; before
assigning new work, changing a milestone boundary, or attempting a live test;
after every accepted or rejected review checkpoint; and before every scheduled
ten-minute user update. Update it immediately after any material result.

## Coordinator authority and modification log

  scraper as needed while preserving safeguards, evidence, and scoped GitHub
  backups.

  must be kept current at the reread points above.
- Jetson changes are authorized when required by the roadmap. Before any
  Jetson modification, locate the appropriate existing Jetson modification
  log; record the exact change, reason, verification, and rollback evidence
  in that file immediately afterward. Do not claim or imply a Jetson change
  until both the system and its record have been updated.

  On 2026-11-19 the corrected, reviewed operator-proof script was added to the
  existing F4 record without executing it; its exact hash, permissions,
  verification, and rollback boundary are recorded in the Jetson changelog,
  register, and dedicated staging receipt.

## Definition of fully completed

Completion means a newly added eligible Company in the Google Sheets database
creates one durable, company-scoped request; the normal scraper runs for that
Company; an existing integration is used or the existing coordinator safely
builds and validates the needed wrapper within its approved capability; the
resulting jobs and evidence are saved through the normal Job Search Helper
path and confirmed in Google Sheets; and the managed first-pass review begins.
The same flow must be restart-safe, avoid duplicate work, preserve manual
choices, expose plain-language status, and pass the final independent
acceptance demonstration. Offline code, a local receipt, a fixture test, or a
partial Google Sheets write alone is not completion.

## Safety boundaries

- Do not create a second repair scheduler. Extend the copied existing
  coordinator only after its contracts and tests are present.
- Do not copy databases, locks, credentials, tokens, runtime histories, or
  private inventories from the protected main checkout.
- Preserve manual decisions, Opportunities, existing cycles, and locks.
- Do not claim live completion from offline tests. Before any live action,
  freshly verify processes, locks, state, source identity, and authorization.
- Save accepted source and test checkpoints to the scoped GitHub branch.

## Next actions

1. Keep the protected queue paused while the final three-company readiness
   matrix is refreshed from current local evidence. Select distinct source
   structures plus the shared-adapter regression control; do not start work
   from historical candidate names alone.
2. The refreshed publisher preflight now shows one pending local Opportunities
   `Last Checked` receipt and 21 protected remote-edit conflicts. Identify the
   pending receipt and reconcile or explicitly retain every conflict under the
   normal safeguards before requesting interactive Google authorization. Do not
   start a scrape.
3. Only after publication is clean, freshly recheck processes, locks,
   local/workbook cycle identity, deployed source identity, and sync health;
   then perform the final demonstration one Company at a time, proving normal
   company-scoped dispatch, wrapper/adapter handling, confirmed Sheets
   publication and managed FIRST PASS start before advancing to the next.

The refreshed matrix is recorded in
`FINAL_ACCEPTANCE_READINESS_20261119.md`. It freezes Forterra/Rippling and
Allen Control/Greenhouse as conditional completion candidates, General
Atomics/BrassRing as the first hold-control, and PDW as the mandatory shared
Rippling regression. General Atomics cannot count as a completed Company while

name absent PIDs `485601`, `634767` and `283795`; the files remain untouched.
Historical broad cycles and the empty automatic profile registry prevent a
safe company-only run until an exact guarded recovery is owner-reviewed.
